# BMad Help

## Purpose

Help the user understand where they are in their BMad workflow and what to do next, and also answer broader questions when asked that could be augmented with remote sources such as module documentation sources.

## Desired Outcomes

When this skill completes, the user should:

1. **Know where they are** — which module and phase they're in, what's already been completed
2. **Know what to do next** — the next recommended and/or required step, with clear reasoning
3. **Know how to invoke it** — skill name, menu code, action context, and any args that shortcut the conversation
4. **Get offered a quick start** — when a single skill is the clear next step, offer to run it for the user right now rather than just listing it
5. **Feel oriented, not overwhelmed** — surface only what's relevant to their current position; don't dump the entire catalog
6. **Get answers to general questions** — when the question doesn't map to a specific skill, use the module's registered documentation to give a grounded answer

## Data Sources

- **Catalog**: `{project-root}/_bmad/_config/bmad-help.csv` — assembled manifest of all installed module skills
- **Config**: Run `uv run {project-root}/_bmad/scripts/resolve_config.py --project-root {project-root}` and use the merged JSON to resolve `output-location` variables and read `core.communication_language` and `modules.bmm.project_knowledge`. The resolver merges `_bmad/config.toml`, `_bmad/config.user.toml`, `_bmad/custom/config.toml`, and `_bmad/custom/config.user.toml` in that order.
- **Artifacts**: Files matching `outputs` patterns at resolved `output-location` paths reveal which steps are possibly completed; their content may also provide grounding context for recommendations
- **Project knowledge**: If `project_knowledge` resolves to an existing path, read it for grounding context. Never fabricate project-specific details.
- **Module docs**: Rows with `_meta` in the `skill` column carry a URL or path in `output-location` pointing to the module's documentation. Fetch and use these to answer general questions about that module.

## CSV Interpretation

The catalog uses this format:

```
module,skill,display-name,menu-code,description,action,args,phase,preceded-by,followed-by,required,output-location,outputs
```

**Phases** determine the high-level flow:
- `anytime` — available regardless of workflow state
- Skills group into folders (`plan`, `ship`; some modules use numbered phases) and flow in order; naming varies by module

**Sequencing** determines recommended ordering within and across phases (these are soft suggestions, not hard gates — see `required` for gating):
- `preceded-by` — skills that should ideally complete before this one
- `followed-by` — skills that should ideally run after this one
- Format: `skill-name` for single-action skills, `skill-name:action` for multi-action skills

**Required gates**:
- `required=true` items must complete before the user can meaningfully proceed to later phases
- A phase with no required items is entirely optional — recommend it but be clear about what's actually required next

**Completion detection**:
- Search resolved output paths for `outputs` patterns and fuzzy-match found files to catalog rows
- Treat a matching output as evidence that the skill started, not that it completed
- Inspect matched artifacts for explicit completion evidence, such as final status or finalization markers; a draft or incomplete marker means the skill is still in progress
- Honor completion stated by the user or established in the current conversation
- When completion cannot be determined reliably, say so and ask the user; do not recommend advancing based on file presence alone

**Descriptions carry routing context** — some contain cycle info and alternate paths (e.g., "back to DS if fixes needed"). Read them as navigation hints, not just display text.

## Response Format

For each recommended item, present:
- `[menu-code]` **Display name** — e.g., "[PR] PRD"
- Skill name in backticks — e.g., `bmad-prd`
- For multi-action skills: action invocation context — e.g., "dev lets run a code review!"
- Description if present in CSV; otherwise your existing knowledge of the skill suffices
- Args if available

**Ordering**: Show optional items first, then the next required item. Make it clear which is which.

## Constraints

- Present all output in `{communication_language}`
- Recommend running each skill in a **fresh context window**
- Match the user's tone — conversational when they're casual, structured when they want specifics
- If the active module is ambiguous, retrieve all meta rows remote sources to find relevant info also to help answer their question


ARGUMENTS: check the sprint status. We ran build story for 1-1, did it complete the build cycle? Are we ready for story 1-2?

# MCP Server Instructions

The following MCP servers have provided instructions for how to use their tools and resources:

## claude.ai Claude Docs
Claude Docs: living docs you create and edit here. A docs skill your client lists → load it before any docs call — also before a `read`, comment or tab change on a claude.ai …/artifact/… link (the link is a doc; never web-fetch it). No docs skill or guide text loaded → `guide( items = ["topic.index"] )` alone before any docs call but a doc's birth. Make a doc here — not a local file, even when coding — only when the user asks for one, and make it FIRST: the turn's first tool call is its skeleton (title, byline, a `pending` block per section) — a reflex: send it before any search, file read, plan, `guide` or thinking it through; think once it is open — `batch( container = {"kind":"project","create":{"name":"<title>","doc":{"blocks":{"asof":{"type":"date","value":"<today>"},"me":{"type":"mention","user":"me"},"s1":{"type":"pending","intent":"Goals: the three outcomes this quarter commits to"},"s2":{…}},"markdown":"# <title>\n\n<?claude block asof?> · <?claude block me?>\n\n<?claude block s1?>\n\n<?claude block s2?>"}}}, batch = [] )` (`<?claude block k?>` ↔ `blocks.k`); its ack links the doc → `open` it with your Artifact tool (none → start your next message with the link, once); they're likely watching it fill — keep them posted in a short line naming what you're on (outline up; now <topic>); findings go in the doc, not chat; then `guide( items = ["topic.index"] )`, research, and fill each section: `replace` its pending id with `## <heading>` + body; end with one line + the link, never the document. Summoned by a doc comment (turn headed `[Artifact comment sent to Claude]`, `;thread=<root id>`): answer ONLY with a doc comment under that root (`create` an utterance, parent `<root id>`) — no artifact/platform comment tool: that relay thread is resolved and never reaches the doc; an edit asked there → `update` with `answering: "<root id>"`.

- bmad-project-context: Set up, adopt, refresh, or audit a repository's agent instructions (the AGENTS.md block) so AI agents work well in that repo. Also records observed agent mistakes as pitfalls. Use when invoked by name
- bmad-qa-generate-e2e-tests: Generate automated API and end-to-end tests for implemented features. Use when the user says "create qa automated tests for [feature]"
- bmad-retrospective: Review a completed epic against the evidence it left behind — spec, stories, diffs, commits, sprint status — and produce a retrospective with sourced findings, action items, and an acceptance decision. Use when the user says "run a retrospective" or "lets retro the epic [epic]". Supports -H/--headless
- bmad-review: Runs one or more installed review lenses — adversarial critique, edge cases, verification gaps, structure, prose — and reports triaged findings. Use when, and only when, the user asks you to review a diff, a pull request, or an artifact — code or documents, one or many — and actually says "review"; an explicit skill:bmad-review directive from another skill counts as that ask. A request to act on feedback from an earlier review is a change, not a review. Never invoke this uninvited, including on edits you just made.
- bmad-spec: Condense any input — an idea, brief, PRD, transcript, or mixed notes — into a short spec: SPEC.md plus supporting files that downstream skills build from. Also updates and validates existing specs, and can break a spec into stories. Use when the user says "create a spec", "distill this into a spec", "validate this spec", "update the spec", or "break this into stories"

- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. When the destination is a first-party document connector (host-designated, never self-described) that renders live charts, hand it the rows (inline, or as an uploaded data file the chart cites) rather than a rendered PNG/SVG — a picture of a chart loses hover, data inspection and per-value comments. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. - code-review: Review the current diff, or a PR number/branch/path target, for correctness bugs (plus reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups where the model's review recipe covers them) at the given effort level (low|medium|high|xhigh|max: from few high-confidence findings up to many, some of them uncertain; ultra: deep multi-agent review in the cloud); with no level given, it reuses the level you typed last. Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review. Pass --max-findings <n> to report up to n findings, or --max-findings all for every finding. The choice stays until you pass --max-findings default. For ultra on a GitHub.com PR target, --post asks to post the finished review’s findings to the PR as a single comment from the user’s GitHub account (not a review; the launch dialog still confirms in interactive sessions, while non-interactive mode posts on the flag alone) and --no-post hides that option.

# STATUS DEFINITIONS:
# ==================
# Epic Status:
#   - backlog: Epic not yet started
#   - in-progress: Epic actively being worked on
#   - done: All stories in epic completed

# Story Status:
#   - backlog: Story only exists in epic file
#   - ready-for-dev: Story file created, ready for development
#   - in-progress: Developer actively working on implementation
#   - review: Implementation complete, ready for review
#   - done: Story completed

# Retrospective Status:
#   - optional: Can be completed but not required
#   - done: Retrospective has been completed

# Action Item Status:
#   - open: Committed during a retrospective, not yet addressed
#   - in-progress: Actively being worked on
#   - done: Completed

# WORKFLOW NOTES:
# ===============
# - Epic transitions to 'in-progress' automatically when its first story starts (via build's sprint sync)
# - Stories can be worked in parallel if team capacity allows
# - Developer typically creates the next story after the previous one is 'done' to incorporate learnings
# - Dev moves story to 'review', then runs code-review (fresh context, different LLM recommended)
# - Retrospective appends its action items to action_items; the status view surfaces open ones

## Review Triage Log

Pass 1 (blind-hunter BH, edge-case-hunter EC, verification-gap VG).

| # | Finding | Verdict | Evidence | Route |
|---|---------|---------|----------|-------|
| EC1 | Path matching no route (`/foo`, `/parks/`) renders an empty aside | medium | `<Routes>` in `src/App.jsx` has only `/` and `/parks/:id`; any other path leaves the Parks region blank with no way back | patch |
| EC2/BH8 | Park with `name: null` renders an empty `<h1>` | low | Real in `ParkDetailsPlaceholder`, but `park-data.md` treats `name` as required and all 12 have one; a fallback label would add a branch and risk inventing a value | reject (unlikely) |
| EC3/BH7 | Duplicate or missing `id` makes a park unreachable | low | `parks.find` returns the first; sample ids are unique and `id` is required per `park-data.md` | reject (unlikely) |
| EC4/BH9 | Header shows "1 parks" / "0 parks" | low | Sample always has 12; pluralization and an empty state add branches | reject (unlikely) |
| EC5 | Array entry passes `typeof === 'object'` and becomes a phantom park | low | True, but the data never contains nested arrays; the fix adds a guard | reject (unlikely) |
| EC6/BH4 | Stem-only image mapping would replace a real image with the same filename | low | Stem mapping is exactly what `park-data.md` specifies; only hypothetical future URLs hit it | reject (unlikely, by design) |
| EC7/BH1 | No AbortController or timeout; a hung fetch shows loading forever, StrictMode double-fetches | low | The `cancelled` flag prevents stale state; a same-origin static file is unlikely to hang; a timeout adds complexity | reject (unlikely) |
| EC8 | Sort test oracle uses a different `localeCompare` than production | low | No case or accent ties among sample names, so both orders agree | reject (unlikely) |
| BH2 | Focus is lost when Retry unmounts | low | Real, but only on the rare error path; focus management adds code; Story 1.5 does the a11y pass | reject (unlikely) |
| BH3 | `hasCoords` accepts out-of-range lat/lng | low | `finiteNumber` only; `lat: 200` gives `hasCoords: true`, which breaks the Story 1.4 markers; "valid lat/lng" in CAP-1 implies range. Direct correction of an existing guard | patch |
| BH5 | `text()` tests the trimmed value but returns the untrimmed string | low | Padded names would sort and number differently; the fix is a direct correction (return the trimmed value) | patch |
| BH6 | No tie-breaker in `byName` | low | No duplicate names in the sample; adding a sort key is extra logic | reject (unlikely) |
| BH10 | Normalization rules and the non-array load path are untested | — | Duplicate of VG1–VG3 | merged into VG |
| BH11 | Byte-identical AC is not automated | false | The spec's Verification uses `cmp`; it ran and passed | reject |
| BH12 | Load error reason is swallowed | low | Diagnostic only; the error UI works | reject (unlikely) |
| BH13 | No `engines` field makes install non-reproducible | false | `package-lock.json` pins versions; vitest 4 and jsdom 29 install cleanly on current Node lines | reject |
| BH14 | `document.title` is not updated per view; favicon 404 | low | Cosmetic; per-view titles fit the Story 1.5 a11y pass | reject (unlikely) |
| BH15 | No `<h1>` during loading or error | low | Transient state; a11y pass in 1.5 | reject (unlikely) |
| VG1 | Null-name ordering in `byName` is untested | medium | Pre-verified: removing the null guards passes all tests, but a nameless record would crash the load | patch |
| VG2 | Non-array payload rejection is untested | medium | Pre-verified: deleting the `Array.isArray` check makes corrupt data look like an empty set, and no test fails | patch |
| VG3 | Whitespace-to-null, `stringList` filtering and negative `acreage` are untested | medium | Pre-verified: simplifying `text()` or dropping `acreage >= 0` passes all tests | patch |
| VG-o | Image-mapping tests assert `.png`, but the build inlines data URIs | false | The tests run in vitest, where `?url` yields file URLs; mapping by stem is what is being tested, and the build passes | reject |

## Design Notes

The park `number` is assigned once at load time and shared by list cards and map markers (Stories 1.2 and 1.4), so it must not be recomputed per view. `rating` stays `null` when it is not a finite number in 0–5, so the UI can never show 0 stars by accident.

## Verification

**Commands:**
- `npm test` -- expected: all tests pass.
- `npm run build` -- expected: succeeds with no errors.
- `cmp requirements/parks.sample.json public/data/parks.sample.json` -- expected: no output.

**Manual checks (if no CLI):**
- `npm run dev`, then open `/` and `/parks/nope`: the landmarks are present, "Park not found" shows with its link, and Tab shows the amber focus ring on the link.

1	# Build New Preview Workflow
2	
3	**Goal:** Turn user intent into a hardened, reviewable artifact.
4	
5	**CRITICAL:** If a step directs you to another snapshot file, read it fully and follow it. No exceptions.
6	
7	Subagents, when the capability is available, are an important part of this workflow. Use them as directed by the workflow steps.
8	If you need an explicit user instruction to run them, ask once now for the whole workflow run.
9	
10	## READY FOR DEVELOPMENT STANDARD
11	
12	A specification is "Ready for Development" when:
13	
14	- **Actionable**: Every task has a file path and specific action.
15	- **Logical**: Tasks ordered by dependency.
16	- **Testable**: All ACs use Given/When/Then.
17	- **Complete**: No placeholders or TBDs.
18	- **Sufficient**: No known requirement, acceptance, dependency, or implementation gaps remain unresolved.
19	- **Coherent**: No unresolved ambiguities or internal contradictions.
20	
21	## SCOPE STANDARD
22	
23	A specification should target a **single user-facing goal** within **900–1600 tokens**:
24	
25	- **Single goal**: One cohesive feature, even if it spans multiple layers/files. Multi-goal means >=2 **top-level independent shippable deliverables** — each could be reviewed, tested, and merged as a separate PR without breaking the others. Never count surface verbs, "and" conjunctions, or noun phrases. Never split cross-layer implementation details inside one user goal.
26	  - Split: "add dark mode toggle AND refactor auth to JWT AND build admin dashboard"
27	  - Don't split: "add validation and display errors" / "support drag-and-drop AND paste AND retry"
28	- **900–1600 tokens**: Optimal range for LLM consumption. Below 900 risks ambiguity; above 1600 risks context-rot in implementation agents.
29	- **Neither limit is a gate.** Both are proposals with user override.
30	
31	## Conventions
32	
33	- Every operational cross-file reference in this workflow is an absolute snapshot path. Open it directly; do not resolve it relative to a skill directory.
34	- `{project-root}`-prefixed paths resolve from the project working directory.
35	- Whenever this workflow captures or records a version-control revision, obtain the full canonical identifier directly from version control and preserve it verbatim.

# Epic 1 Context: Find a Park

<!-- Compiled from planning artifacts. Edit freely. Regenerate with compile-epic-context if planning docs change. -->

## Goal

Deliver a small, keyless web app (a 2-hour take-home) that lets a resident find a local park in the NYC-area sample set on a map or in a list and open its details, on phone or desktop, by mouse, touch, or keyboard, with missing data handled honestly. The interviewer must be able to run it with one install and one start command and judge it from the README. A modest app that works and is well explained beats an ambitious one with broken core features; stop at 2 hours and document what is unfinished.

## Stories

- Story 1.1: Scaffold app and park data layer
- Story 1.2: Park list with keyboard selection
- Story 1.3: Park details with missing-data handling
- Story 1.4: Map of parks synced with selection
- Story 1.5: Responsive and accessibility polish with design applied
- Story 1.6: Tests and submission README

## Requirements & Constraints

- **Cross-cutting (every story):** no geolocation ever requested; no credentials or API keys; never invent missing data; `parks.sample.json` is never edited (copied into the app byte-for-byte); keyboard focus always visible.
- The list plus details view must be a complete alternative to the map. All 12 parks appear in the list, including ones with missing fields; parks without valid lat/lng get no marker but stay in the list and details.
- Every one of the 12 parks renders; null/missing/empty fields are omitted or labelled, never crash the UI. Failed images degrade without a broken-image icon.
- A data-load failure shows an error message with retry, never a blank screen.
- At 375px and desktop widths, list → details → back works with no horizontal scroll.
- Unredacted AI transcripts (Claude Code, exported by the developer before each fresh story context, and Claude Design) and design files are part of the submission; the README must say where they live.
- Non-goals: backend, accounts, deployment, data editing. Search, filters, current location only if core is done with time to spare.
- Where the design and the spec disagree, the spec wins.

## Technical Decisions

- **Stack:** React + Vite, Tailwind CSS (mobile-first), `react-leaflet` + Leaflet with OpenStreetMap standard tiles (keyless, attribution visible), Vitest + React Testing Library. No component library.
- **Data loading (readiness decision):** the spec says static import, but loading/error/retry states require a mechanism that can actually fail. Use one that can (e.g. `fetch` from `public/` or a dynamic `import()`) and document the choice in the README.
- **Routing (readiness decision):** React Router. `/` is the list, `/parks/:id` is details (supersedes the design's `?park=` option). Unknown id shows "park not found" with a way back. Browser Back must work.
- **Data shape:** array of park objects with `id`, `name` (treat as required); `description`, `location.{lat,lng,address}`, `amenities` (kebab-case tokens), `hours` (free text, shown as written), `images` (URL array), `acreage`, `rating` (0–5) may each be missing, null, or empty. Read nested fields null-safely (`park.location?.address`).
- **Normalization:** one null-safe module applies safe defaults with no fabricated values, sorts parks A–Z and numbers them 1–N; that number is shared by list cards and map markers. Centralize fallback rules in a small helper (e.g. `display(value, fallback)`).
- **Image URL transformation:** pixel art moves from `design/park-images/` to `src/assets/park-images/`. At load time each image URL maps by filename stem to the bundled asset (`.../prospect-1.jpg` → `park-images/prospect-1.png`); unmatched URLs pass through unchanged so `onError` fallback still applies. Do not mutate source data. Document in README.
- **Amenity labels:** replace `-` with a space and capitalize the first letter (`dog-run` → "Dog run"); unknown tokens are humanized, never dropped.
- **Map:** initial view fits bounds of parks with coordinates. Leaflet default marker icons need explicit asset imports under Vite (or use `L.divIcon` with a `<button>` inside). Markers are focusable, named "N. Park name"; the selected marker is amber and larger.
- **Tailwind tokens:** paper `#F6F4EE`, surface `#FFFFFF`, line `#DAD6CB`, ink `#1B2620`, muted `#4F5B54`, forest `#1E5B3F` / dark `#123826`, tag bg `#E9EFE7` / fg `#23472F`, amber `#B45309`, missing bg `#F0EDE5` / line `#C9C4B6`. Font: Public Sans (Google Fonts, 400–800). Focus ring: `focus-visible:outline focus-visible:outline-[3px] focus-visible:outline-amber focus-visible:outline-offset-2`.
- **Tests must cover** every known data gap (see UX section), a failing image, an unmatched image URL, the URL transformation without mutation, and the keyboard list → details → back flow.

## UX & Interaction Patterns

- **Landmarks:** `<header>`, `<main>`, `<aside aria-label="Parks">`, map region labelled "Map of parks". One `<h1>` per view; no clickable `<div>`s; icon-only buttons have `aria-label`s; touch targets ≥ 44×44px; text contrast ≥ 4.5:1.
- **Desktop (≥ 768px):** green header (app name "Find a Park", subtitle, park count); ~400px independently scrolling sidebar; map fills the rest. Selecting a park switches the sidebar to details, starting with an "All parks" back control.
- **Phone (< 768px):** single column with a List / Map toggle of two `<button>`s using `aria-pressed`, List default. Tapping a marker shows a preview card ("Park N of 12", name, address, hours, "View park details", close button "Close park preview"). Details take the full screen with an "All parks" back link.
- **Focus management:** opening details moves focus to the details heading; returning to the list moves focus to the originating card.
- **Park card:** single `<button>`/link per card (one Tab stop) in a `<ul>`; number badge, name (17px bold), address, first three amenities as tags plus "+N more".
- **Details order:** back control, photo area ("Photo 1 of N", alt text naming the park), name as `<h1>` (26–28px, 800), address, "Open in OpenStreetMap" link from lat/lng, `<dl>` fact tiles (Hours, Size in acres, Rating "4.7 / 5"), About, all amenities as checked tags.
- **Missing-data copy:** address → "Address not listed" (muted); description null → "No description has been provided for this park."; rating null → "Not yet rated" (never 0/empty stars); empty or failed images → dashed box "No photos for this park yet"; hours/acreage → "Not listed"; empty amenities → hide section or "No amenities listed"; no lat/lng → no marker, no OSM link. Missing fact tiles use the muted style (missing bg, dashed border, italic), as on the phone screen; the desktop mock's normal-style "Not yet rated" tile is a mistake.
- **Sample gaps:** Old Mill Botanical Garden (null description), Cedar Hill Nature Preserve (empty images, null rating; coordinates look like a placeholder, note in Known issues), East Ridge Trailhead (empty images), Highland Dog Park (missing address).
- **Loading/error states:** "Loading parks…" and "Couldn't load parks. Try again." with a retry button, using the card style.
- Visual reference: `design/screens/` (1280×800 and 390×844). `design/source/*.dc.html` is reference only, not app code.

## Cross-Story Dependencies

- 1.1 provides the data layer, routing, layout shell, tokens, and bundled images that all later stories build on.
- 1.2 needs the 1.3 back control for its focus-return-to-card criterion. **Readiness decision:** in 1.2, verify focus return using browser Back; 1.3 confirms it via the "All parks" control.
- 1.4 relies on the shared A–Z numbering from 1.1 and the selected-park route to highlight markers and open details.
- 1.5 is the full responsive/accessibility pass over 1.2–1.4 (which ship baseline accessibility).
- 1.6 tests cover behaviour from 1.1–1.3; the README records decisions from all stories, including the data-load mechanism and React Router choice.
<frozen-after-approval reason="human-owned intent — do not modify unless human renegotiates">

## Intent

**Problem:** There is no app yet. Every later story needs a runnable Vite + React + Tailwind project, park data that cannot crash the UI, routes, a layout shell, and bundled park images.

**Approach:** Scaffold the app and fetch `parks.sample.json` from `public/` through a null-safe loader. The loader normalizes the data, sorts parks A–Z, numbers them 1–N and maps image URLs to bundled art. Wrap it in a loading/error/retry hook, and render landmark regions with placeholder content for routes `/` and `/parks/:id`.

## Boundaries & Constraints

**Always:** one `npm install` + `npm run dev`; no API keys; `requirements/parks.sample.json` copied byte-for-byte and never edited; normalization never fabricates values and never mutates the parsed source; nested reads null-safe; tokens, the Public Sans font and the amber focus ring come from `design/README.md`.

**Never:** list cards, details content, a map, or responsive layout work (Stories 1.2–1.5); geolocation; a component library; a static JSON import (it cannot fail, so the error state would be fiction; this decision came from the readiness gate).

## I/O & Edge-Case Matrix

| Scenario | Input / State | Expected Output / Behavior | Error Handling |
|----------|--------------|---------------------------|----------------|
| Happy path | 12 sample parks | 12 parks sorted A–Z by name, `number` 1–12 | N/A |
| Missing nested field | Highland: no `location.address` | `address: null`, no throw | N/A |
| Null / empty fields | Old Mill `description: null`; Cedar Hill `images: []`, `rating: null` | stay `null` / `[]`; no defaults invented | N/A |
| Missing arrays | `amenities` or `images` absent | `[]` | N/A |
| Bad coordinates | lat/lng missing or non-finite | `hasCoords: false`; park kept | N/A |
| Image mapped | `https://images.example.com/prospect-1.jpg` | bundled `prospect-1.png` URL | N/A |
| Image unmatched | `https://x.test/zzz.jpg` | URL unchanged | `onError` fallback later (1.3) |
| Load fails | fetch rejects or returns non-OK | "Couldn't load parks. Try again." + Retry button | Retry refetches |
| Loading | fetch pending | "Loading parks…" | N/A |
| Unknown route id | `/parks/nope` | "Park not found" + link back to `/` | N/A |

</frozen-after-approval>

## Code Map

- Greenfield repo: only `README.md`, `.gitignore` (already ignores `node_modules`, `dist`; `public` is not ignored), `design/`, `requirements/`, and transcripts. Do not touch `design/screens`, `design/source`, the transcripts or `_bmad*`.
- `requirements/parks.sample.json` -- source data; copy it to `public/data/parks.sample.json`.
- `design/park-images/*.png` (12 files) -- `git mv` them to `src/assets/park-images/`; the stems match all 12 sample URLs.
- `design/README.md` -- tokens, font, focus-ring classes, landmark names, loading/error copy.
- Toolchain: Node 25, npm 11. Current releases: vite 8, @vitejs/plugin-react 6, tailwindcss + @tailwindcss/vite 4 (CSS `@theme`, no config file), react 19, react-router 8, vitest 5.

## Tasks & Acceptance

**Execution:**
- [x] `package.json`, `vite.config.js`, `index.html` -- Vite + React + `@tailwindcss/vite`; scripts `dev`, `build`, `test` (`vitest run`); add the Vitest jsdom config and RTL setup; load Public Sans 400–800 from Google Fonts in `index.html`; set the title to "Find a Park".
- [x] `src/index.css` -- `@import "tailwindcss"`; put the `@theme` colour tokens and font from `design/README.md` here and define a `focus-ring` utility with the amber focus-visible outline.
- [x] `public/data/parks.sample.json` -- byte copy of the requirements file.
- [x] `src/assets/park-images/` -- moved pixel art.
- [x] `src/data/parks.js` -- `normalizePark`, `normalizeParks` (sort A–Z, assign `number`), `resolveImageUrl` (stem lookup via `import.meta.glob(..., { eager: true, query: '?url', import: 'default' })`), `loadParks()` (fetch `${import.meta.env.BASE_URL}data/parks.sample.json`, throw on non-OK). Normalized shape: `{ id, name, number, description, address, lat, lng, hasCoords, amenities, hours, images, acreage, rating }`. Use `null` for missing scalars and `[]` for missing arrays.
- [x] `src/data/useParks.js` -- hook returning `{ status: 'loading'|'error'|'ready', parks, retry }`.
- [x] `src/App.jsx`, `src/main.jsx` -- `BrowserRouter`; routes `/` and `/parks/:id`. The shell renders `<header>` (app name, park count once ready), `<main>`, `<aside aria-label="Parks">` (placeholder: the "All parks" heading on `/`; the park name, or "Park not found" with a link back, on `/parks/:id`) and `<section aria-label="Map of parks">` (placeholder). Loading and error states render inside the aside.
- [x] `src/data/parks.test.js`, `src/App.test.jsx` -- unit-test every I/O matrix row, including that the source object is not mutated; test the loading → error → retry → ready flow by mocking `fetch`.

**Acceptance Criteria:**
- Given a fresh clone, when `npm install` and `npm run dev` run, then the app serves locally with no keys.
- Given the app at `/parks/prospect-park`, when the user presses browser Back after navigating from `/`, then `/` renders again.
- Given `public/data/parks.sample.json`, when compared with `requirements/parks.sample.json`, then the bytes are identical.

## Implementation Notes

- vitest 4.1 + jsdom 29 instead of vitest 5 + jsdom 30: the latter declare engines that exclude Node 25 (odd release), so plain `npm install` on Node 25 picks v4/v29 and installs warning-free. Upgrade when on Node 24 LTS or 26.
- Park PNGs are under Vite's 4 KB inline limit, so `?url` yields data URIs in the production build (bundled, no separate files).
- Tests read the fixture with `process.cwd()` because jsdom gives `import.meta.url` an http scheme.
- Normalization also treats empty/whitespace strings as `null` and out-of-range `acreage` (< 0) as `null`; non-object entries are skipped.

75:### Story 1.2: Park list with keyboard selection
76-

78-I want to browse every park in a list and open one without using the map,
79-So that I can find a park by mouse, touch, or keyboard alone.

import { Link, Route, Routes, useParams } from 'react-router'
import { useParks } from './data/useParks.js'

const cardClass = 'rounded-[10px] border border-line bg-surface p-4'
const linkClass = 'focus-ring rounded text-forest underline'

function ParkListPlaceholder() {
  return <h1 className="text-lg font-bold">All parks</h1>
}

function NotFound() {
  return (
    <div>
      <h1 className="text-lg font-bold">Park not found</h1>
      <p className="mt-2">
        <Link to="/" className={linkClass}>
          Back to all parks
        </Link>
      </p>
    </div>
  )
}

function ParkDetailsPlaceholder({ parks }) {
  const { id } = useParams()
  const park = parks.find((p) => p.id === id)

  if (!park) return <NotFound />

  return <h1 className="text-[28px] font-extrabold">{park.name}</h1>
}

function ParksPanel({ status, parks, retry }) {
  if (status === 'loading') {
    return (
      <p role="status" className={cardClass}>
        Loading parks…
      </p>
    )
  }

  if (status === 'error') {
    return (
      <div role="alert" className={cardClass}>
        <p>Couldn't load parks. Try again.</p>
        <button
          type="button"
          onClick={retry}
          className="focus-ring mt-3 min-h-11 rounded-lg bg-forest px-4 font-semibold text-white hover:bg-forest-dark"
        >
          Retry
        </button>
      </div>
    )
  }

  return (
    <Routes>
      <Route path="/" element={<ParkListPlaceholder />} />
      <Route path="/parks/:id" element={<ParkDetailsPlaceholder parks={parks} />} />
      <Route path="*" element={<NotFound />} />
    </Routes>
  )
}

export default function App() {
  const { status, parks, retry } = useParks()

  return (
    <div className="flex min-h-screen flex-col">
      <header className="bg-forest px-5 py-4 text-white">
        <p className="text-xl font-extrabold">Find a Park</p>
        <p className="text-sm">New York City area</p>
        {status === 'ready' && <p className="text-sm">{parks.length} parks</p>}
      </header>
      <main className="flex flex-1 flex-col">
        <aside aria-label="Parks" className="p-5">
          <ParksPanel status={status} parks={parks} retry={retry} />
        </aside>
        <section aria-label="Map of parks" className="flex-1 p-5">
          <p className="text-muted">Map coming soon.</p>
        </section>
      </main>
    </div>
  )
}
@import "tailwindcss";

/* Design tokens from design/README.md */
@theme {
  --color-paper: #F6F4EE;
  --color-surface: #FFFFFF;
  --color-line: #DAD6CB;
  --color-ink: #1B2620;
  --color-muted: #4F5B54;
  --color-forest: #1E5B3F;
  --color-forest-dark: #123826;
  --color-tag-bg: #E9EFE7;
  --color-tag-fg: #23472F;
  --color-amber: #B45309;
  --color-missing-bg: #F0EDE5;
  --color-missing-line: #C9C4B6;

  --font-sans: "Public Sans", "Segoe UI", system-ui, sans-serif;
}

/* Amber focus ring: apply `focus-ring` to every interactive element. */
@utility focus-ring {
  &:focus-visible {
    outline-style: solid;
    outline-width: 3px;
    outline-color: var(--color-amber);
    outline-offset: 2px;
  }
}

body {
  @apply bg-paper text-ink font-sans;
}
import { useCallback, useEffect, useState } from 'react'
import { loadParks } from './parks.js'

/** Loads parks once; `retry` refetches after an error. */
export function useParks() {
  const [attempt, setAttempt] = useState(0)
  const [state, setState] = useState({ status: 'loading', parks: [] })

  useEffect(() => {
    let cancelled = false
    setState({ status: 'loading', parks: [] })
    loadParks().then(
      (parks) => {
        if (!cancelled) setState({ status: 'ready', parks })
      },
      () => {
        if (!cancelled) setState({ status: 'error', parks: [] })
      },
    )
    return () => {
      cancelled = true
    }
  }, [attempt])

  const retry = useCallback(() => setAttempt((n) => n + 1), [])

  return { status: state.status, parks: state.parks, retry }
}

As a resident,
I want to see everything known about a park, and a clear note where information is missing,
So that I can decide whether to visit without being misled.

20-Screenshots are at 2x: desktop at 1280×800, phone at 390×844. The map in every screenshot is a drawn stand-in. The real app uses Leaflet with OpenStreetMap tiles.

22-## Layout and behaviour
23-
--
28-  - **Left sidebar (~400px):** scrolls on its own.
29-  - **Map:** fills the rest of the screen.
30:- **Sidebar, list mode:** an "All parks" heading, a one-line hint, then one card per park.
31-- **Selecting a park** from either the list or the map:
32-  - The sidebar switches to details mode, starting with an "All parks" back button.
33-  - That park's marker turns amber and gets bigger.
34-  - The URL should change too (e.g. `?park=prospect-park` or `/parks/:id`), so details can be linked and the browser Back button works.
35:- **Going back to the list:** move focus to the card of the park you came from.
36-- **Opening details:** move focus to the details heading, so keyboard and screen-reader users land in the right place.
37-
38-### Phone (< 768px)
39-
40-- One column. A **List / Map** toggle sits under the header, made of two real `<button>`s with `aria-pressed`. List is the default.
41:- **Tapping a marker** opens a preview card at the bottom of the map. It shows the park's position in the list ("Park 8 of 12"), its name, address and hours, a **View park details** button and a close button.
42-- **Details** take the whole screen, with an "All parks" back link in the header.
43-
44-### List ↔ map link
45-
46:- Parks are sorted A–Z and numbered 1–12. The same number appears on the list card and on the map marker, so the two views always match.
47-- Every marker is focusable and named "8. Prospect Park" for screen readers. Use `L.divIcon` with a `<button>` inside it, or set `keyboard: true` and `alt`/`title` on the markers.
48-- The list is the main way to choose a park. The map is never the only way.
49-
50:## Park card (list)
51-
52:- Number badge, park name (17px bold), address, and the first three amenities as tags. If there are more, add a "+N more" tag.
53:- The whole card is a single `<button>` (or a link once there is routing), so it gets keyboard focus and one Tab stop.
54-
55-## Details
56-
57-The details view shows, from top to bottom:
58-
59-1. Photo area (see the missing-data rules below)
60-2. Park name as the page heading (26–28px, 800 weight), then the address
61-3. An **Open in OpenStreetMap** link built from the park's lat/lng (`https://www.openstreetmap.org/?mlat={lat}&mlon={lng}#map=16/{lat}/{lng}`). This was added during design and is easy to drop if you don't want it.
62-4. Three fact tiles: **Hours**, **Size** (acres), **Rating** ("4.7 / 5")
63-5. **About** (the description)
64:6. **Amenities**: all of them, as tags with a check mark. Turn the raw values into labels by replacing `-` with a space and capitalising the first letter (`dog-run` → "Dog run").
65-
66-## Missing data: never invent a value
67-
68-The brief requires this, and the sample data has every one of these cases.

71-|---|---|---|
72-| `location.address` | Highland Dog Park | "Address not listed" in the secondary text colour |

// Null-safe park data layer. Normalization never invents values and never
// mutates the parsed source: missing scalars become `null`, missing arrays `[]`.

const imageModules = import.meta.glob('../assets/park-images/*.png', {
  eager: true,
  query: '?url',
  import: 'default',
})

// filename stem (e.g. "prospect-1") -> bundled asset URL
const bundledImages = Object.fromEntries(
  Object.entries(imageModules).map(([path, url]) => [fileStem(path), url]),
)

function fileStem(pathOrUrl) {
  const withoutQuery = pathOrUrl.split(/[?#]/)[0]
  const file = withoutQuery.split('/').pop() ?? ''
  const dot = file.lastIndexOf('.')
  return dot > 0 ? file.slice(0, dot) : file
}

/** Map a source image URL to the bundled art with the same filename stem; unmatched URLs pass through unchanged. */
export function resolveImageUrl(url) {
  if (typeof url !== 'string') return url
  return bundledImages[fileStem(url)] ?? url
}

function text(value) {
  if (typeof value !== 'string') return null
  const trimmed = value.trim()

Each card is a single React Router `<Link>` to `/parks/:id`. Keep the loading/error states, `NotFound`, `cardClass`, and the landmarks.\n- `src/data/parks.js` -- normalized park: `{ id, name, number, address, amenities, ... }`; `amenities` is always an array of strings. Don't change normalization.\n- `src/index.css` -- tokens (`tag-bg`, `tag-fg`, `muted`, `forest`, `line`, `surface`) and the `focus-ring` utility.\n- `src/App.test.jsx` -- `renderAt(path)` + `okResponse()` + the `TestNav` pattern for history Back; extend it here.\n- `design/screens/01-desktop-list-map.png`, `04-phone-list.png` -- card visual: forest round number badge, 17px bold name, muted address, rounded tags. On mount, if `focusParkId` matches a card, focus that link.\n- [ ] `src/App.jsx` -- keep the last-opened park id in App-level state (set from the details route), pass it to `ParkList`, and clear it after the list has used it. The details route records the id on mount. The list reads it once on mount, focuses that card, then clears it, so a later fresh visit to `/` does not steal focus. Story 1.3's \"All parks\" control will rely on the same mechanism (epic readiness decision).\n\nThe card's accessible name is all of its text (number, name, address, tags). That is acceptable for one link per card and matches the design's single-element card.\n\n## Verification\n\n**Commands:**\n- `npm test` -- expected: all tests pass.\n- `npm run build` -- expected: succeeds with no errors.\n\n**Manual checks (if no CLI):**\n- `npm run dev`, Tab through the list: amber ring on each card; Enter opens details; browser Back puts focus back on that card.\n"

===== 2026-10-15T05:24:19.352000+00:00  [main]  assistant =====
The plan for Story 1-2 is written and needs your approval before I write any code. It's in `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-1-2-park-list-with-keyboard-selection.md` (about 1,400 tokens, one goal, no open questions).

**What gets built:**
- **List page:** the `/` page gets an "All parks" heading, a one-line hint ("Choose a park to see hours, amenities and directions."), and a list of all 12 parks.
- **Park cards:** each card is one link to `/parks/:id`, so it's a single Tab stop with the amber focus ring. Enter, click or tap opens the park's page.
- **Card contents:** number badge, name, and address. Highland Dog Park shows "Address not listed" in the muted grey. Each card shows up to 3 amenity tags plus "+N more" when there are more.
- **Readable tags:** a new shared helper in `src/data/format.js` turns `dog-run` into "Dog run". Story 1.3 will reuse it.
- **Focus coming back:** when you return to the list from a park's page, focus lands back on that park's card (action item 3). Browser Back doesn't carry router state, so the app itself remembers the last park opened. The list moves focus to that card once, then forgets it. Opening `/` directly doesn't focus any card.

**Left for later stories:** the park page content, its "All parks" back control and moving focus to its heading (1.3), the map (1.4), and the desktop/phone layout (1.5).

**Tests:** every case in the spec's table, Tab order, Enter and click opening a park, and focus coming back after Back.

---

Before approving, you can open the spec file in an editor or ask me questions and tell me what to change. You can also use `bmad-advanced-elicitation` or `bmad-party-mode`, ideally in another session to avoid context bloat.

---

How would you like to proceed?
- **Approve and continue:** I write the code in this session.
- **Approve and stop:** the spec is marked `ready-for-dev`, and a fresh `bmad-build` session picks it up for coding.
- **Review spec:** a reviewer checks the spec first and we go over changes together.

14	
15	## Intent
16	
17	**Problem:** The `/` route shows only an "All parks" heading. A resident cannot browse parks or open one without a map, and the list is the main way to choose a park (CAP-2).
18	
19	**Approach:** Replace the list placeholder with an "All parks" heading, a one-line hint, and a `<ul>` of 12 park cards, sorted A–Z by the existing loader. Each card is a single React Router `<Link>` to `/parks/:id`. When the user comes back to `/` from a park, focus returns to that park's card.
20	
21	## Boundaries & Constraints
22	
23	**Always:** use the normalized parks from `useParks` as they are (order and `number` come from the loader, never recomputed); never invent data; one Tab stop per card; amber `focus-ring` on every card; touch target ≥ 44px; Tailwind tokens from `src/index.css`.
24	
25	**Never:** details content, the "All parks" back control or focus-to-heading on details (Story 1.3); map or markers (1.4); desktop sidebar/phone toggle layout (1.5); clickable `<div>`s; a nested interactive element inside a card.
26	
27	## I/O & Edge-Case Matrix
28	
29	| Scenario | Input / State | Expected Output / Behavior | Error Handling |
30	|----------|--------------|---------------------------|----------------|
31	| Happy path | 12 sample parks | 12 `<li>` cards in loader order, badges 1–12 | N/A |
32	| Missing address | Highland Dog Park `address: null` | "Address not listed" in `text-muted` | N/A |
33	| > 3 amenities | Prospect Park (7) | first 3 tags + "+4 more" | N/A |
34	| ≤ 3 amenities | East Ridge (3) | 3 tags, no "+N more" | N/A |
35	| No amenities | `amenities: []` | no tag row | N/A |
36	| Amenity label | `dog-run`, `wifi` | "Dog run", "Wifi" | unknown tokens humanized, never dropped |
37	| Return from details | `/parks/highland-dog-park` → Back | Highland card has focus | N/A |
38	| First visit | open `/` directly | no card is auto-focused | N/A |
39	
40	</frozen-after-approval>
41	
42	## Code Map
43	
44	- `src/App.jsx` -- `ParkListPlaceholder` (renders only the h1) is what this story replaces; `ParksPanel` owns the `<Routes>` and receives `parks`; `ParkDetailsPlaceholder` renders on `/parks/:id`. Keep the loading/error states, `NotFound`, `cardClass`, and the landmarks.
45	- `src/data/parks.js` -- normalized park: `{ id, name, number, address, amenities, ... }`; `amenities` is always an array of strings. Don't change normalization.
46	- `src/index.css` -- tokens (`tag-bg`, `tag-fg`, `muted`, `forest`, `line`, `surface`) and the `focus-ring` utility.
47	- `src/App.test.jsx` -- `renderAt(path)` + `okResponse()` + the `TestNav` pattern for history Back; extend it here.
48	- `design/screens/01-desktop-list-map.png`, `04-phone-list.png` -- card visual: forest round number badge, 17px bold name, muted address, rounded tags. Hint copy: "Choose a park to see hours, amenities and directions."
49	
50	## Tasks & Acceptance
51	
52	**Execution:**
53	- [ ] `src/data/format.js` -- `amenityLabel(token)`: replace `-` with spaces, capitalize the first letter, keep the rest; shared with 1.3's details.
54	- [ ] `src/components/ParkList.jsx` -- `ParkList({ parks, focusParkId })`: h1 "All parks", hint `<p>`, `<ul>` of `<li><Link to={`/parks/${id}`}>` cards (badge, name, address or muted fallback, up to 3 tags + "+N more"). On mount, if `focusParkId` matches a card, focus that link.
55	- [ ] `src/App.jsx` -- keep the last-opened park id in App-level state (set from the details route), pass it to `ParkList`, and clear it after the list has used it. Delete `ParkListPlaceholder`.
56	- [ ] `src/data/format.test.js`, `src/App.test.jsx` -- unit-test `amenityLabel`; test every matrix row, Tab reaching each card link, Enter and click navigating to details, and focus returning via history Back.
57	
58	**Acceptance Criteria:**
59	- Given the list, when a keyboard user presses Tab, then each card receives focus once, as a link, with the `focus-ring` class.
60	- Given a focused card, when the user presses Enter, then the URL becomes `/parks/:id` and that park's heading renders.
61	- Given a card, when the user clicks or taps it, then the app navigates to that park's details.
62	- Given the list renders, then all 12 parks appear inside a single `<ul>` under the "All parks" h1 and its hint.
63	
64	## Design Notes
65	
66	Focus return has to work with browser Back, which carries no router state, so the id of the park the user came from lives in App state rather than in `location.state`. The details route records the id on mount. The list reads it once on mount, focuses that card, then clears it, so a later fresh visit to `/` does not steal focus. Story 1.3's "All parks" control will rely on the same mechanism (epic readiness decision).
67	
68	The card's accessible name is all of its text (number, name, address, tags). That is acceptable for one link per card and matches the design's single-element card.
69

73	- `npm test` -- expected: all tests pass.
74	- `npm run build` -- expected: succeeds with no errors.
75	
76	**Manual checks (if no CLI):**
77	- `npm run dev`, Tab through the list: amber ring on each card; Enter opens details; browser Back puts focus back on that card.

The following MCP servers have provided instructions for how to use their tools and resources:

## claude.ai Claude Docs
Claude Docs: living docs you create and edit here. A docs skill your client lists → load it before any docs call — also before a `read`, comment or tab change on a claude.ai …/artifact/… link (the link is a doc; never web-fetch it). No docs skill or guide text loaded → `guide( items = ["topic.index"] )` alone before any docs call but a doc's birth. Make a doc here — not a local file, even when coding — only when the user asks for one, and make it FIRST: the turn's first tool call is its skeleton (title, byline, a `pending` block per section) — a reflex: send it before any search, file read, plan, `guide` or thinking it through; think once it is open — `batch( container = {"kind":"project","create":{"name":"<title>","doc":{"blocks":{"asof":{"type":"date","value":"<today>"},"me":{"type":"mention","user":"me"},"s1":{"type":"pending","intent":"Goals: the three outcomes this quarter commits to"},"s2":{…}},"markdown":"# <title>\n\n<?claude block asof?> · <?claude block me?>\n\n<?claude block s1?>\n\n<?claude block s2?>"}}}, batch = [] )` (`<?claude block k?>` ↔ `blocks.k`); its ack links the doc → `open` it with your Artifact tool (none → start your next message with the link, once); they're likely watching it fill — keep them posted in a short line naming what you're on (outline up; now <topic>); findings go in the doc, not chat; then `guide( items = ["topic.index"] )`, research, and fill each section: `replace` its pending id with `## <heading>` + body; end with one line + the link, never the document. Summoned by a doc comment (turn headed `[Artifact comment sent to Claude]`, `;thread=<root id>`): answer ONLY with a doc comment under that root (`create` an utterance, parent `<root id>`) — no artifact/platform comment tool: that relay thread is resolved and never reaches the doc; an edit asked there → `update` with `answering: "<root id>"`.

While auto mode is active:

You can do much of your work through the Bash tool when it is the simpler route: read files with cat, head, or sed -n, search with grep and find, and make small, mechanical file changes with sed, heredocs, or short scripts instead of the dedicated Read, Edit, or Write tools. # Epic 1 Context: Find a Park

<!-- Compiled from planning artifacts. Edit freely. Regenerate with compile-epic-context if planning docs change. -->

## Goal

Deliver a small, keyless web app (a 2-hour take-home) that lets a resident find a local park in the NYC-area sample set on a map or in a list and open its details, on phone or desktop, by mouse, touch, or keyboard, with missing data handled honestly. The interviewer must be able to run it with one install and one start command and judge it from the README. A modest app that works and is well explained beats an ambitious one with broken core features; stop at 2 hours and document what is unfinished.

## Stories

- Story 1.1: Scaffold app and park data layer
- Story 1.2: Park list with keyboard selection
- Story 1.3: Park details with missing-data handling
- Story 1.4: Map of parks synced with selection
- Story 1.5: Responsive and accessibility polish with design applied
- Story 1.6: Tests and submission README

## Requirements & Constraints

- **Cross-cutting (every story):** no geolocation ever requested; no credentials or API keys; never invent missing data; `parks.sample.json` is never edited (copied into the app byte-for-byte); keyboard focus always visible.
- The list plus details view must be a complete alternative to the map. All 12 parks appear in the list, including ones with missing fields; parks without valid lat/lng get no marker but stay in the list and details.
- Every one of the 12 parks renders; null/missing/empty fields are omitted or labelled, never crash the UI. Failed images degrade without a broken-image icon.
- A data-load failure shows an error message with retry, never a blank screen.
- At 375px and desktop widths, list → details → back works with no horizontal scroll.
- Unredacted AI transcripts (Claude Code, exported by the developer before each fresh story context, and Claude Design) and design files are part of the submission; the README must say where they live.
- Non-goals: backend, accounts, deployment, data editing. Search, filters, current location only if core is done with time to spare.
- Where the design and the spec disagree, the spec wins.

## Technical Decisions

- **Stack:** React + Vite, Tailwind CSS (mobile-first), `react-leaflet` + Leaflet with OpenStreetMap standard tiles (keyless, attribution visible), Vitest + React Testing Library. No component library.
- **Data loading (readiness decision):** the spec says static import, but loading/error/retry states require a mechanism that can actually fail. Use one that can (e.g. `fetch` from `public/` or a dynamic `import()`) and document the choice in the README.
- **Routing (readiness decision):** React Router. `/` is the list, `/parks/:id` is details (supersedes the design's `?park=` option). Unknown id shows "park not found" with a way back. Browser Back must work.
- **Data shape:** array of park objects with `id`, `name` (treat as required); `description`, `location.{lat,lng,address}`, `amenities` (kebab-case tokens), `hours` (free text, shown as written), `images` (URL array), `acreage`, `rating` (0–5) may each be missing, null, or empty. Read nested fields null-safely (`park.location?.address`).
- **Normalization:** one null-safe module applies safe defaults with no fabricated values, sorts parks A–Z and numbers them 1–N; that number is shared by list cards and map markers. Centralize fallback rules in a small helper (e.g. `display(value, fallback)`).
- **Image URL transformation:** pixel art moves from `design/park-images/` to `src/assets/park-images/`. At load time each image URL maps by filename stem to the bundled asset (`.../prospect-1.jpg` → `park-images/prospect-1.png`); unmatched URLs pass through unchanged so `onError` fallback still applies. Do not mutate source data. Document in README.
- **Amenity labels:** replace `-` with a space and capitalize the first letter (`dog-run` → "Dog run"); unknown tokens are humanized, never dropped.
- **Map:** initial view fits bounds of parks with coordinates. Leaflet default marker icons need explicit asset imports under Vite (or use `L.divIcon` with a `<button>` inside). Markers are focusable, named "N. Park name"; the selected marker is amber and larger.
- **Tailwind tokens:** paper `#F6F4EE`, surface `#FFFFFF`, line `#DAD6CB`, ink `#1B2620`, muted `#4F5B54`, forest `#1E5B3F` / dark `#123826`, tag bg `#E9EFE7` / fg `#23472F`, amber `#B45309`, missing bg `#F0EDE5` / line `#C9C4B6`. Font: Public Sans (Google Fonts, 400–800). Focus ring: `focus-visible:outline focus-visible:outline-[3px] focus-visible:outline-amber focus-visible:outline-offset-2`.
- **Tests must cover** every known data gap (see UX section), a failing image, an unmatched image URL, the URL transformation without mutation, and the keyboard list → details → back flow.

## UX & Interaction Patterns

- **Landmarks:** `<header>`, `<main>`, `<aside aria-label="Parks">`, map region labelled "Map of parks". One `<h1>` per view; no clickable `<div>`s; icon-only buttons have `aria-label`s; touch targets ≥ 44×44px; text contrast ≥ 4.5:1.
- **Desktop (≥ 768px):** green header (app name "Find a Park", subtitle, park count); ~400px independently scrolling sidebar; map fills the rest. Selecting a park switches the sidebar to details, starting with an "All parks" back control.
- **Phone (< 768px):** single column with a List / Map toggle of two `<button>`s using `aria-pressed`, List default. Tapping a marker shows a preview card ("Park N of 12", name, address, hours, "View park details", close button "Close park preview"). Details take the full screen with an "All parks" back link.
- **Focus management:** opening details moves focus to the details heading; returning to the list moves focus to the originating card.
- **Park card:** single `<button>`/link per card (one Tab stop) in a `<ul>`; number badge, name (17px bold), address, first three amenities as tags plus "+N more".
- **Details order:** back control, photo area ("Photo 1 of N", alt text naming the park), name as `<h1>` (26–28px, 800), address, "Open in OpenStreetMap" link from lat/lng, `<dl>` fact tiles (Hours, Size in acres, Rating "4.7 / 5"), About, all amenities as checked tags.
- **Missing-data copy:** address → "Address not listed" (muted); description null → "No description has been provided for this park."; rating null → "Not yet rated" (never 0/empty stars); empty or failed images → dashed box "No photos for this park yet"; hours/acreage → "Not listed"; empty amenities → hide section or "No amenities listed"; no lat/lng → no marker, no OSM link. Missing fact tiles use the muted style (missing bg, dashed border, italic), as on the phone screen; the desktop mock's normal-style "Not yet rated" tile is a mistake.
- **Sample gaps:** Old Mill Botanical Garden (null description), Cedar Hill Nature Preserve (empty images, null rating; coordinates look like a placeholder, note in Known issues), East Ridge Trailhead (empty images), Highland Dog Park (missing address).
- **Loading/error states:** "Loading parks…" and "Couldn't load parks. Try again." with a retry button, using the card style.
- Visual reference: `design/screens/` (1280×800 and 390×844). `design/source/*.dc.html` is reference only, not app code.

## Cross-Story Dependencies

- 1.1 provides the data layer, routing, layout shell, tokens, and bundled images that all later stories build on.
- 1.2 needs the 1.3 back control for its focus-return-to-card criterion. **Readiness decision:** in 1.2, verify focus return using browser Back; 1.3 confirms it via the "All parks" control.
- 1.4 relies on the shared A–Z numbering from 1.1 and the selected-park route to highlight markers and open details.
- 1.5 is the full responsive/accessibility pass over 1.2–1.4 (which ship baseline accessibility).
- 1.6 tests cover behaviour from 1.1–1.3; the README records decisions from all stories, including the data-load mechanism and React Router choice.

import { Link, Route, Routes, useParams } from 'react-router'
import { useParks } from './data/useParks.js'

const cardClass = 'rounded-[10px] border border-line bg-surface p-4'
const linkClass = 'focus-ring rounded text-forest underline'

function ParkListPlaceholder() {
  return <h1 className="text-lg font-bold">All parks</h1>
}

function NotFound() {
  return (
    <div>
      <h1 className="text-lg font-bold">Park not found</h1>
      <p className="mt-2">
        <Link to="/" className={linkClass}>
          Back to all parks
        </Link>
      </p>
    </div>
  )
}

function ParkDetailsPlaceholder({ parks }) {
  const { id } = useParams()
  const park = parks.find((p) => p.id === id)

  if (!park) return <NotFound />

  return <h1 className="text-[28px] font-extrabold">{park.name}</h1>
}

function ParksPanel({ status, parks, retry }) {
  if (status === 'loading') {
    return (
      <p role="status" className={cardClass}>
        Loading parks…
      </p>
    )
  }

  if (status === 'error') {
    return (
      <div role="alert" className={cardClass}>
        <p>Couldn't load parks. Try again.</p>
        <button
          type="button"
          onClick={retry}
          className="focus-ring mt-3 min-h-11 rounded-lg bg-forest px-4 font-semibold text-white hover:bg-forest-dark"
        >
          Retry
        </button>
      </div>
    )
  }

  return (
    <Routes>
      <Route path="/" element={<ParkListPlaceholder />} />
      <Route path="/parks/:id" element={<ParkDetailsPlaceholder parks={parks} />} />
      <Route path="*" element={<NotFound />} />
    </Routes>
  )
}

export default function App() {
  const { status, parks, retry } = useParks()

  return (
    <div className="flex min-h-screen flex-col">
      <header className="bg-forest px-5 py-4 text-white">
        <p className="text-xl font-extrabold">Find a Park</p>
        <p className="text-sm">New York City area</p>
        {status === 'ready' && <p className="text-sm">{parks.length} parks</p>}
      </header>
      <main className="flex flex-1 flex-col">
        <aside aria-label="Parks" className="p-5">
          <ParksPanel status={status} parks={parks} retry={retry} />
        </aside>
        <section aria-label="Map of parks" className="flex-1 p-5">
          <p className="text-muted">Map coming soon.</p>
        </section>
      </main>
    </div>
  )
}

// Null-safe park data layer. Normalization never invents values and never
// mutates the parsed source: missing scalars become `null`, missing arrays `[]`.

const imageModules = import.meta.glob('../assets/park-images/*.png', {
  eager: true,
  query: '?url',
  import: 'default',
})

// filename stem (e.g. "prospect-1") -> bundled asset URL
const bundledImages = Object.fromEntries(
  Object.entries(imageModules).map(([path, url]) => [fileStem(path), url]),
)

function fileStem(pathOrUrl) {
  const withoutQuery = pathOrUrl.split(/[?#]/)[0]
  const file = withoutQuery.split('/').pop() ?? ''
  const dot = file.lastIndexOf('.')
  return dot > 0 ? file.slice(0, dot) : file
}

/** Map a source image URL to the bundled art with the same filename stem; unmatched URLs pass through unchanged. */
export function resolveImageUrl(url) {
  if (typeof url !== 'string') return url
  return bundledImages[fileStem(url)] ?? url
}

function text(value) {
  if (typeof value !== 'string') return null
  const trimmed = value.trim()
  return trimmed !== '' ? trimmed : null
}

function finiteNumber(value) {
  return typeof value === 'number' && Number.isFinite(value) ? value : null
}

function numberInRange(value, min, max) {
  const n = finiteNumber(value)
  return n !== null && n >= min && n <= max ? n : null
}

function stringList(value) {
  return Array.isArray(value) ? value.filter((item) => text(item) !== null) : []
}

export function normalizePark(raw) {
  const park = raw && typeof raw === 'object' ? raw : {}
  const location = park.location && typeof park.location === 'object' ? park.location : {}
  const lat = numberInRange(location.lat, -90, 90)
  const lng = numberInRange(location.lng, -180, 180)
  const acreage = finiteNumber(park.acreage)
  const rating = finiteNumber(park.rating)

  return {
    id: text(park.id),
    name: text(park.name),
    number: null,
    description: text(park.description),
    address: text(location.address),
    lat,
    lng,
    hasCoords: lat !== null && lng !== null,
    amenities: stringList(park.amenities),
    hours: text(park.hours),
    images: stringList(park.images).map(resolveImageUrl),
    acreage: acreage !== null && acreage >= 0 ? acreage : null,
    rating: rating !== null && rating >= 0 && rating <= 5 ? rating : null,
  }
}

function byName(a, b) {
  if (a.name === null) return b.name === null ? 0 : 1
  if (b.name === null) return -1
  return a.name.localeCompare(b.name, 'en', { sensitivity: 'base' })
}

/** Normalize, sort A–Z by name and number 1–N. The number is assigned once here and shared by every view. */
export function normalizeParks(rawParks) {
  if (!Array.isArray(rawParks)) return []
  return rawParks
    .filter((raw) => raw && typeof raw === 'object')
    .map(normalizePark)
    .sort(byName)
    .map((park, index) => ({ ...park, number: index + 1 }))
}

export async function loadParks() {
  const response = await fetch(`${import.meta.env.BASE_URL}data/parks.sample.json`)
  if (!response.ok) {
    throw new Error(`Failed to load parks: HTTP ${response.status}`)
  }
  const data = await response.json()
  if (!Array.isArray(data)) {
    throw new Error('Failed to load parks: expected an array')
  }
  return normalizeParks(data)
}

import { useEffect, useRef } from 'react'
import { Link } from 'react-router'
import { amenityLabel } from '../data/format.js'

const MAX_TAGS = 3

function ParkCard({ park, linkRef }) {
  const shown = park.amenities.slice(0, MAX_TAGS)
  const extra = park.amenities.length - shown.length

  return (
    <li>
      <Link
        ref={linkRef}
        to={`/parks/${park.id}`}
        className="focus-ring flex min-h-11 items-start gap-3 rounded-[10px] border border-line bg-surface p-4 hover:border-forest"
      >
        <span
          aria-hidden="true"
          className="flex size-8 shrink-0 items-center justify-center rounded-full bg-forest text-sm font-bold text-white"
        >
          {park.number}
        </span>
        <span className="sr-only">{`${park.number}. `}</span>
        <span className="min-w-0 flex-1">
          <span className="block text-[17px] font-bold">{park.name}</span>{' '}
          {park.address ? (
            <span className="block text-muted">{park.address}</span>
          ) : (
            <span className="block text-muted">Address not listed</span>
          )}{' '}
          {shown.length > 0 && (
            <span className="mt-2 flex flex-wrap gap-2">
              {shown.map((token) => (
                <span key={token} className="rounded-full bg-tag-bg px-2.5 py-0.5 text-sm text-tag-fg">
                  {amenityLabel(token)}
                </span>{' '}
              ))}
              {extra > 0 && (
                <span className="rounded-full bg-tag-bg px-2.5 py-0.5 text-sm text-tag-fg">
                  +{extra} more
                </span>
              )}
            </span>
          )}
        </span>
        <svg
          aria-hidden="true"
          viewBox="0 0 24 24"
          className="size-5 shrink-0 self-center text-muted"
          fill="none"
          stroke="currentColor"
          strokeWidth="2"
        >
          <path d="m9 6 6 6-6 6" />
        </svg>
      </Link>
    </li>
  )
}

/**
 * The "All parks" list. Parks are rendered in loader order with loader numbers.
 * If `focusParkId` matches a card on mount, that card's link gets focus and
 * `onFocusUsed` is called so a later fresh visit does not steal focus.
 */
export default function ParkList({ parks, focusParkId, onFocusUsed }) {
  const linkRefs = useRef(new Map())

  useEffect(() => {
    if (!focusParkId) return
    linkRefs.current.get(focusParkId)?.focus()
    onFocusUsed?.()
    // Run once on mount: the id is read once, then cleared by the parent.
    // eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps
  }, [])

  return (
    <div>
      <h1 className="text-lg font-bold">All parks</h1>

  Story 1.2 (park list with keyboard selection) is implemented as the spec describes. `npm test` passes (49/49) and `npm run build` succeeds. I did not run the manual check in a real browser: Tab through the cards in `npm run dev`, check the amber ring, press Enter, then use browser Back.
  
  I read the spec's one context file, `epic-1-context.md`, before starting.
  
  **What changed**
  - **New `src/data/format.js`:** `amenityLabel(token)` swaps `-` for spaces and capitalizes the first letter, so `dog-run` becomes "Dog run". Unknown tokens are humanized, never dropped.
  - **New `src/components/ParkList.jsx`:**
    - Shows the "All parks" h1, the hint text from the design, and a single `<ul>` of 12 cards in the order the loader returns them.
    - Each card is one `<Link>` to `/parks/:id` with the `focus-ring` class and a minimum height of 44px. It holds the round forest-green number badge, the name at 17px bold, and the address or "Address not listed" in muted text.

    - On mount, if `focusParkId` matches a card, that card's link gets focus and `onFocusUsed` is called.
  - **`src/App.jsx`:**
    - `ParkListPlaceholder` is deleted, and the App component now holds `lastParkId` in state.
    - The details route records the park id when it mounts, but only for a park that exists.
    - `ParkList` gets the id and a callback that clears it after use, so a later fresh visit to `/` doesn't steal focus.
    - Loading/error/retry, `NotFound`, `cardClass` and the landmarks are unchanged.

    - one link and no nested buttons per card, with `focus-ring`, the 44px class and the right `href`
    - the missing-address fallback in muted text
    - "+4 more" on Prospect Park; no "+N more" on East Ridge
    - no tag row when amenities are empty (using a modified copy of the sample data)
    - the "Dog run" and "Wifi" labels
    - no card focused on a first visit
    - each card is exactly one Tab stop
    - Enter and click both open the details page
    - history Back returns focus to the Highland card, and the id is cleared so a later visit to `/` doesn't refocus it
  
  **Additions the spec didn't spell out**
  - **Extra prop on `ParkList`:** it takes `onFocusUsed` as well as the `parks` and `focusParkId` the spec lists, so the list can clear the id after using it.

Without them the link's accessible name ran together ("1.Cedar Hill…RdTrails…"); it now reads "1. Cedar Hill Nature Preserve Cedar Hill Rd Trails …".
  - **Chevron:** each card has a decorative chevron icon (hidden from screen readers), taken from the phone design.
  
  **Risks and gaps**
  - **44px touch target:** this is checked only by the presence of the `min-h-11` class; jsdom can't measure layout.
  - **Cards with the same amenity twice:** tags use their label as the React key, so a duplicate amenity in one park would give a duplicate-key warning. The sample data has none.

@@ -0,0 +1,94 @@
+import { Fragment, useEffect, useRef } from 'react'
+import { Link } from 'react-router'
+import { amenityLabel } from '../data/format.js'
+
+const MAX_TAGS = 3
+
+function ParkCard({ park, linkRef }) {
+  const shown = park.amenities.slice(0, MAX_TAGS)
+  const extra = park.amenities.length - shown.length
+  const tags = shown.map(amenityLabel)
+  if (extra > 0) tags.push(`+${extra} more`)
+
+  return (
+    <li>
+      <Link
+        ref={linkRef}
+        to={`/parks/${park.id}`}
+        className="focus-ring flex min-h-11 items-start gap-3 rounded-[10px] border border-line bg-surface p-4 hover:border-forest"
+      >
+        <span
+          aria-hidden="true"
+          className="flex size-8 shrink-0 items-center justify-center rounded-full bg-forest text-sm font-bold text-white"
+        >
+          {park.number}
+        </span>
+        <span className="sr-only">{park.number}.</span>{' '}
+        <span className="min-w-0 flex-1">
+          <span className="block text-[17px] font-bold">{park.name}</span>{' '}
+          {park.address ? (
+            <span className="block text-muted">{park.address}</span>
+          ) : (
+            <span className="block text-muted">Address not listed</span>
+          )}{' '}
+          {tags.length > 0 && (
+            <span className="mt-2 flex flex-wrap gap-2">
+              {tags.map((label) => (
+                <Fragment key={label}>
+                  <span className="rounded-full bg-tag-bg px-2.5 py-0.5 text-sm text-tag-fg">
+                    {label}
+                  </span>{' '}
+                </Fragment>
+              ))}
+            </span>
+          )}
+        </span>
+        <svg
+          aria-hidden="true"
+          viewBox="0 0 24 24"
+          className="size-5 shrink-0 self-center text-muted"
+          fill="none"
+          stroke="currentColor"
+          strokeWidth="2"
+        >
+          <path d="m9 6 6 6-6 6" />
+        </svg>
+      </Link>
+    </li>
+  )
+}
+
+/**
+ * The "All parks" list. Parks are rendered in loader order with loader numbers.
+ * If `focusParkId` matches a card on mount, that card's link gets focus and
+ * `onFocusUsed` is called so a later fresh visit does not steal focus.
+ */
+export default function ParkList({ parks, focusParkId, onFocusUsed }) {
+  const linkRefs = useRef(new Map())
+
+  useEffect(() => {
+    if (!focusParkId) return
+    linkRefs.current.get(focusParkId)?.focus()
+    onFocusUsed?.()
+    // Run once on mount: the id is read once, then cleared by the parent.
+  }, [])
+
+  return (
+    <div>
+      <h1 className="text-lg font-bold">All parks</h1>
+      <p className="mt-1 text-muted">Choose a park to see hours, amenities and directions.</p>
+      <ul className="mt-4 flex flex-col gap-3">
+        {parks.map((park) => (
+          <ParkCard
+            key={park.id}
+            park={park}
+            linkRef={(node) => {
+              if (node) linkRefs.current.set(park.id, node)
+              else linkRefs.current.delete(park.id)
+            }}
+          />
+        ))}
+      </ul>
+    </div>
+  )
+}

@@ -0,0 +1,5 @@
+/** Human label for a kebab-case amenity token: `dog-run` → "Dog run". Unknown tokens are humanized, never dropped. */
+export function amenityLabel(token) {
+  const spaced = String(token ?? '').replace(/-/g, ' ')
+  return spaced.charAt(0).toUpperCase() + spaced.slice(1)
+}

+import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest'
+import { amenityLabel } from './format.js'
+
+describe('amenityLabel', () => {
+  it('replaces dashes with spaces and capitalizes the first letter', () => {
+    expect(amenityLabel('dog-run')).toBe('Dog run')
+    expect(amenityLabel('wildlife-viewing')).toBe('Wildlife viewing')
+  })
+
+  it('capitalizes single-word tokens', () => {
+    expect(amenityLabel('wifi')).toBe('Wifi')
+  })
+
+  it('humanizes unknown tokens instead of dropping them', () => {
+    expect(amenityLabel('zip-line-tower')).toBe('Zip line tower')
+  })
+
+  it('keeps the rest of the token as written', () => {
+    expect(amenityLabel('BBQ-pits')).toBe('BBQ pits')
+  })
+})

70	   For each finding:
71	   - A gap finding from the verification-gap layer arrives pre-verified — that layer's evidence rules made it read the tests and run the searches it cites, and triage trusts the claim as filed.
